Venice → Charles V · homophonic alphabet with a three-letter nomenclator · 1522

Alonso Sánchez at Venice to Charles V, 1522

Go to the full text ↓

Real Academia de la Historia, Salazar 9/23–9/26 · DECODE R9593–R9657 · the imperial ambassador at Venice reporting to the Emperor through the Italian War of 1521–26

Catalogued here as twenty-nine ciphertexts with no decipherment. Satoshi Tomokiyo reconstructed and published the key in September 2025. Three letters are now transcribed from contemporary decipherments filed on their DECODE records, checked against the cipher with his key (R9635, R9613, R9641); two more are decoded with the key but not read (R9653, R9593); 43 records have not been read (twenty-two triaged on 12 November 2026); five letters are read in part with the key unchanged (R9607 72.1% of its words sure, R9610, R9614, R9640 and R9647 58–63%). The work also corrected the catalogue entry, found the alphabetical ordering rule of the nomenclator and the numeral codes it predicts, and separated a second correspondent’s cipher.

Daniel Bourdeau · posted 20 September 2026 · updated 12 November 2026

Method · R9635 (contemporary decipherment f. 22r): read from existing decipherment (complete) · R9653 (Tomokiyo's key applied; decoded, not read): read with known key (partial) · R9593 (Tomokiyo's key applied; decoded, not read): read with known key (partial) · R9607 (Tomokiyo's key applied; read in part): read with known key (partial) · R9610 (Tomokiyo's key applied; read in part): read with known key (partial) · R9614 (second copy of R9613; read in part): read with known key (partial) · R9640 (Tomokiyo's key applied; read in part): read with known key (partial) · R9647 (Tomokiyo's key applied; read in part): read with known key (partial) · On the map

Credits · Brice Dudout (transcription, verification) · Satoshi Tomokiyo (key or decipherment used, earlier solution) · Contemporary key or decipherment (key or decipherment used) · Gustav Adolph Bergenroth (key or decipherment used)

At a glanceKnown key applied · partial (76% of the cipher)

The letters
The catalogue entry lists twenty-nine ciphertexts of Alonso Sánchez, imperial ambassador at Venice, to Charles V, 1522, in the Real Academia de la Historia (Salazar 9/23–9/26); the DECODE run itself holds 52 Sánchez records (Lope Hurtado’s excluded), and nine further records are by Lope Hurtado de Mendoza.
Where
DECODE R9593–R9657, recorded as Non-decrypted; the Inline Cleartext: Yes flag turned out to mark clear opening and closing paragraphs; a contemporary decipherment was found on DECODE only for R9605, R9613, R9635 and R9641.
The cipher
A substitution alphabet with a nomenclature of about 177 three-letter codes (consonant–vowel–consonant) that sort into alphabetical order of their plaintext.
How it was read
With Satoshi Tomokiyo’s key, published 6 September 2025; no cryptanalysis was needed. Here it was applied to R9653 and R9593 (both decoded, not read) and to five letters transcribed blind from the signs and read in part (R9607 72.1% of its words sure, R9610, R9614, R9640 and R9647 58–63%), and used to check the readings of R9635, R9613 and R9641 taken from their contemporary decipherments. Before R9607 the key was tested once on a held-out passage of R9632 ll. 7–25, which it passed (z = 5.68); a second test, of four code values proposed from R9607 and R9610, failed and none was added. Tomokiyo’s article prints only the opening lines of R9593–R9632, so the passages read here lie beyond what it prints.
What was added
The catalogue entry corrected in three ways; the ordering rule, which fills the numeral run and was confirmed in the text (cef = xxvi, cif = xxvii).
Still open
Forty-three Sánchez records (R9594 onward in date order) have not been read (twenty-two of them were triaged on 12 November, see below); the Lope Hurtado letters use a third, related cipher, not yet reconstructed.

01What was already published, and when

Satoshi Tomokiyo, Correspondence in Cipher of Imperial Ambassadors Alonso Sanchez and Juan Manuel (1522), posted on Cryptiana on 6 September 2025 and enlarged on 9 September, reconstructs the key. He worked from the contemporary decipherment attached to DECODE R9605, and describes how he began: browsing R9613 he noticed that vog and xig were frequent and so likely common monosyllables, and that a q with a double bar was often affixed to a code and might be the plural -s; he was then stuck on the handwriting of the decipherment. Turning to R9605 he saw vog veg near the end, two codes close together in an alphabetical listing and both frequent, and matched them to en el in the plaintext. After that, as he puts it, there was no real difficulty.

The article gives the substitution alphabet as a hand-drawn table, the nomenclature, and the deciphered first lines of R9593–R9632, plus the whole of R9621 and the last paragraph of R9623. It also reconstructs the parallel cipher of Juan Manuel, imperial ambassador at Rome (DECODE R9499–R9529), which is built to the same design with different values.

For the prior-solution record: a published key and partial decipherment existed a year before this work, it was found before the attempt, and everything below uses it. Bergenroth’s Calendar (1866) also abstracts fourteen of these letters from the contemporary decipherings; that was found after R9635 had been deciphered in part.

02What the catalogue entry got wrong

The entry read “RAH, Signatura 9/24 (29 items)”, 1522–23, and justified its importance by the negotiation that brought Venice out of the French alliance and ended in the treaty of July 1523. The fifty-five DECODE record pages R9603–R9657, tabulated, contradict all three parts.

  • The shelfmark. The run is spread over Salazar 9/23, 9/24, 9/25 and 9/26. R9603–R9618 are in 9/24, R9619–R9632 in 9/25, R9633–R9657 in 9/26, and Tomokiyo’s list reaches back into 9/23.
  • The dates. Every record carries a 1522 date, the last of them November 1522. Nothing in this group reaches 1523, so the treaty of July 1523 is outside it. The letters belong to the year of La Bicocca, the loss of Genoa and the fall of Rhodes; the Venetian realignment came the year after.
  • The authorship. Nine records are by Lope Hurtado de Mendoza, not Sánchez: R9634, R9644, R9645, R9646, R9648, R9649, R9650, R9652, R9656.

A further correction matters more. DECODE marks almost every record in the group Inline Cleartext: Yes, but when all 163 images were opened on 23 October 2026 that flag proved to mark clear opening and closing paragraphs and postscripts: a bound contemporary decipherment was found only for R9605, R9613, R9635 and R9641. The Calendar of State Papers Spain, vol. 2, calendars many of these same letters with the note “Autograph in cipher. Contemporary deciphering”, so many were deciphered at the time, but for most of them that decipherment is not on DECODE. What they lack is an edition. DECODE’s Non-decrypted means that no decipherment is entered in the database; the catalogue entry took it to mean that none exists.

03The cipher, and the rule that orders it

The system is the 1496 Ferdinand-and-Isabella type: a homophonic substitution alphabet, three nulls that letters open with, a q with a double bar for the plural -s, and a nomenclator of three-letter codes for common words, names and places. Word division is kept. The vocabulary is large for the date.

Tomokiyo’s table, set out as data, shows that the codes follow a pattern. They are consonant–vowel–consonant over

  • first position, sixteen consonants z y x v t s r p n m l h g f d c b, running backwards through the alphabet as the plaintext runs forwards;
  • middle, the five vowels a e i o u;
  • third position, nine consonants b c d f g h l m n.

That is 720 slots, of which about 177 were recovered. Sorting by (first consonant, third consonant, vowel) puts the plaintext in alphabetical order: zid zod zud | zef zif | zog zug | zel | zem zom | zen zun gives a(h)un, ahun, al, allende, algun, alla, alli, antes, aqui, aquell, arma, assi. The alphabetisation is the clerk’s own and is loose at the edges (allende before algun, cosa before concorda), so what the rule gives for an unassigned code is a range, not a point. Where the plaintext is a numeral series, the range collapses to a value.

04The numeral series, predicted and then confirmed

Tomokiyo has cob xviii, cub xix, cad xx through cud xxiv, and caf xxv, and leaves the rest of the block blank, with a query on cud and an uncertain “[xxviii?]” in his reading of R9598. The ordering rule fills the run:

codecabcebcibcobcubcadcedcidcodcudcafcefcifcofcuf
valuexvxvixviixviiixixxxxxixxiixxiiixxivxxvxxvixxviixxviiixxix

The two in bold were predicted before they were seen, and then met in the foot paragraph of R9653 in a context that can only be a pair of dates (next section). That in turn resolves the uncertain “[xxviii?]” of R9598 as cof. The predicted values are carried in key_codes.tsv flagged as this project’s, separately from Tomokiyo’s.

05R9653: the fall of Rhodes, in clear, with a ciphered postscript

R9653 is Sánchez to Charles V, Venice, 20 November 1522, Salazar 9/26 f. 299–301. It is beyond the end of Tomokiyo’s list and is not calendared in CSP Spain 2. Folio 299r is a mixed leaf: six lines of cipher at the head, some thirty lines in clear, two lines of cipher at the foot, then the signature.

The clear middle, transcribed from the image:

A xiij del presente fue la postrera que escreui a v[uest]ra m[agesta]d y dixe las nueuas de Rodas que aqui hauia dicho vn patron de vn nauio; despues [vinie]ron cartas y los d’esta republica me embiaron vn sumario, cuyo traslado sera con esta. Esta manyana han houido otras cartas y son de Candia de su general de xx del passado, y las tenia el de Rodas de x del mismo. … Lo que dizen es que hasta x de octubre hauian dado los turquos cinco conbates muy rezios a Rodas por diuersos lugares, y baterias, y por debaxo de tierra por las minas y por debaxo de la agua, y siempre los hauian rebotado y muerto gran numero dellos — dizen xx mil. Pero vn secretario de la Señoria me ha dicho a mi que despues que el turquo esta sobre Rodas se le han muerto cinquenta mil hombres en las batallas y de dolencia; su armada de mar estaua muy mal en horden, y algunos hauisos hai que la persona del turquo se ha retirado y passado en tierra firme. … Dizen que los de Rodas esperan socorro con desseo, y que si les llegasen mil infantes ternian por muy cierta la victoria. No hai duda sino que ahora hauria buena disposicion de hazer muy gran daño al turquo y a su armada.

Rhodes capitulated a month after this was written, on 22 December 1522.

The cipher, 23 October 2026. The images were reopened: f. 299v carries more cipher, and the cipher of the foot of f. 299r and of f. 299v was deciphered sign by sign with Tomokiyo’s key (R9653_reading.md, R9653_cipher.txt). The first paragraph builds a sentence (“[?Este] embaxador y ahun la señoria tienen cartas de Francia de xxvi y xxvii del passado de [?Sangerman]”), but the second, on f. 299v ll. 7–12 (Adorno, the protonotary, Juan Manuel), does not: its code groups xug, zi, tac, lud, qef, sob, ca and nu are absent from Tomokiyo’s table and met once here. No decipherment of R9653 is bound or imaged, so it stays decoded, not read. Its second copy (f. 300v) is seen only as bleed-through, and R9654, of the same date, has no images on DECODE.

The second line runs yeh+plural xig tom xig cef y cif xel mog vuc xig …: “cartas de Francia de xxvi y xxvii del passado de …”: letters out of France of 26 and 27 October, the month in which Francis I’s position in Lombardy collapsed. It is this pair of dates that confirms cef and cif. The first line gives ta es, vam embaxa(dor), vuc+plural, zid a(h)un, rug la, gub señor, vog en, around spelled-out letters not yet settled.

A small corroboration from the other end of the group: Lope Hurtado’s letter of 9 November (R9648) opens in clear “La carta de V. M[agestad] recebi de xxvj de octubre”, the same post that Sánchez reports reaching Venice.

06R9635: a full page of cipher, deciphered in part

R9635 is Sánchez to Charles V, Venice, 16 September 1522, Salazar 9/26 f. 20–22, calendared in CSP Spain vol. 2 p. 482 and also beyond Tomokiyo’s list. Folio 20r is the best ciphertext page in the group: about twenty-five lines of cipher, whole-page, in a clean well-spaced hand, with a contemporary decipherment written between the lines in lighter ink. The decipherment covers only patches, so most lines have to be decrypted with the key.

cipherreading
xof+s yeh+s xig dom puddas cartas de vuestra magestad
pac+s xig vum gɣ lallos de esta republica
xel gub racdel señor infante
vud+s zef xig pac+s mud+s cob pol9ducados allende de los otros xviii mil
ped xig cab pol9 vud+smas de xv mil ducados
faf+s pac+s … pol9 vud+s pic xig vomtodos los … mil ducados luego de esto
interlinear glossobligados … fuessen restituydas sus bienes

The subject is money and reprisals: the sums owed by the Signoria under the capitulation (eighteen thousand ducats outstanding from a previous year, more than fifteen thousand in another reckoning) and, in the glossed lines, goods seized that were to be restored to their owners. That is the same business as the sequestrations of imperial vassals’ goods at Venice which fill R9638 (11 October 1522) entirely in clear, and the same reckoning of xx mil ducados allende de xviii mil that Tomokiyo deciphered in the last paragraph of R9623.

Two points of notation, which the page settles: pol9 is pol (mi) followed by the letter sign for l, giving mil; that sign is easily taken for a g in this hand; and gɣ lal is the spelled letters r-e followed by lal (publica), giving republica, the word these despatches turn on.

This page also attests cab = xv, in mas de xv mil ducados, the third of the numerals predicted from the ordering rule, after cef and cif in R9653. None of the seven is contradicted.

The decipherment of the time, found 23 October 2026. Besides the interlinear patches, the DECODE record holds a full contemporary decipherment of the letter: a clear court-hand copy on f. 22r (image P3, right page). The reading now follows it, checked against the cipher of ff. 20r–20v with the key (R9635_reading.md, English R9635_en.md). A tear across f. 22r ll. 9–11 was filled from the cipher of f. 20r ll. 11–14, read sign by sign (hec = quiera, vac = dicha, gta = l + es, which settles the token that had resisted). It opens:

Visto que [?ya] tanto tardavan cartas de vuestra magestad, pues en esto no se puede perder nada, fuy con los desta republica y dixeles que ya sabian que por la capitulacion de la tregua que con vuestra magestad tienen assentada, el primero deste mes havian de pagar otros xx mil ducados, allende de los otros xviij mil que deven del otro año; que les rogava quisiessen pagarlos y cumplir con lo que son obligados.

Four words of the decipherment stay uncertain in the cramped hand ([?bastar], [?tenido], [?Menorami], [?Lucerna]). The decipherment makes hec quiera where f. 20r had suggested Inglaterra above.

Three more letters, 23 October 2026

  • R9613, Venice, 18 June 1522 (Salazar 9/24 ff. 203–207): read from its contemporary decipherment, a clear copy on ff. 206r–207v of the same DECODE record, never opened before (R9613_reading.md); its first sentence checked word for word against the cipher of f. 203r (hug = recebi). “Recebi la carta de vuestra magestad del postrero del passado, y fuy con los desta republica …”: Charles V is well disposed to peace with Venice on honest and reasonable terms. CSP Spain 2 no. 429 abstracts the same letter.
  • R9641 (with its duplicate R9642), Venice, 27 October 1522 (Salazar 9/26 ff. 173–188): read from its contemporary decipherment on ff. 187r–188r, filed on DECODE under R9642 as “Sánchez?” (R9641_reading.md); checked against the cipher of ff. 173r and 174r (“de todo esto tengo dado aviso al duque de Sessa”; new value vad = duque). The Venetians are content with the Pope’s answer to their complaint about the imperial army’s quarters in the Ghiara d’Adda.
  • R9593, Venice, 5 March 1522 (Salazar 9/23 f. 27): read sign by sign with the key on the two DECODE images (R9593_reading.md): letters of Don Juan Manuel and Geronimo Adorno sent on, the lansquenets’ passage of Val Camonica, the French taking Caravaggio, the Swiss at Monza. Two values adopted (gim = suyos, fef = toda), but about twenty groups stay open and the end of f. 27r and lines of f. 27v do not build: decoded, not read. CSP Spain 2 does not calendar it; the next letter, R9594 (15 March), is CSP no. 396, which Bergenroth abstracted from a contemporary decipherment while calling the cipher “not extant”.

R9607 and a test of the key on new text, 12 November 2026

Brice Dudout triaged eleven DECODE records of this run (R9594, R9607, R9616, R9617, R9626, R9631, R9632, R9633, R9636, R9639, R9651; 55 images). Two pairs are one letter in two copies (R9616/R9617; R9626 with R9627), DECODE serves R9632’s second opening twice, R9636 and R9651 are mixed with full clear passages, and R9594 (calendared as CSP 396) has a surviving contemporary decipherment. Only R9607 and R9633 are uncalendared. None carries a decipherment imaged on its record. The other ten records are triaged here, not read.

The key, tested once on new text. Before decoding anything, the test was fixed and its files hashed (by his record, the hash list was written before the measure): this folder’s key, unchanged, on R9632 f. 200r lines 7–25, 386 tokens transcribed blind by sign shape, with R9632 used for no key adjustment. The score is the mean four-gram log-probability per character on a Don Quijote model, against 400 controls (200 permuted keys, 200 token shuffles). Thresholds set in advance: at least 300 characters, above every control, z ≥ 5. Result: 593 decoded characters at −2.471 per character against controls with mean −3.106 and best −2.766, z = 5.68, so the test passed. Re-run for this page with the same figures. The decode reads “despues los de esta republica nos enbiaron a llamar al embaxador de Inglaterra … para respondernos a lo que postreramente les …”, the matter of CSP 473. It shows that the key turns a new passage into Spanish; the 4-gram score cannot say that every word is right.

R9607, Venice, 14 May 1522 (Salazar 9/24 ff. 65r–67v; DECODE R9607), not calendared in CSP Spain 2, no decipherment imaged. Transcribed blind by sign shape (146 lines), frozen, decoded with the key unchanged and read by sense. It is read in part: of 1,555 words, 1,121 are sure (72.1%), 116 probable (look-alike signs such as g = r or y, or P = f or s, settled by sense), 2 restored and 316 unresolved; his own count, sure plus probable, is 79.5% (79.9% after a re-check by a rule frozen beforehand, which settled five more words as probable and left the sure words at 1,121). Most unresolved words are spelled runs mis-transcribed on look-alike signs; 63 code groups absent from the key are listed, none added. Sentences build on every page: Venice arming galleys after the French defeat, with more in Candia; French soldiers come from Milan to Padua; Cremona, Lautrec and Prospero Colonna; Sánchez’s demand that the Signoria declare itself released from its capitulation with France; the Turk’s fleet reported from Corfu; the Hungarian ambassador’s departure; the Signoria’s written answer, “esta bien conforme a aquello”. Tomokiyo’s printed opening line (“A la carta de vuestra magestad de [xxix?] de abril …”) agrees. Not checked here: the transcriptions against the DECODE images (login needed). The key is the one already used above, so there is one key and one entry. Files: dudout/.

Four more letters, and a second test that failed, 12 November 2026

The same contributor then worked through the next DECODE lot (17 records, 43 images; TRIAGE_lot2.md). Every Sánchez letter in it is uncalendared and has no decipherment imaged, except R9624, marked “triplicada”, a third copy of the 7 August letter (R9626/R9627); R9628 is a copy of R9629, R9612 is mixed with long clear passages, R9657 is a letter of Lope Hurtado de Mendoza, and R9606, R9637 and R9655 have no image online. Four letters were read, each transcribed blind by sign shape and frozen before decoding, with the key unchanged. Counted by the README rule, only the words whose letters and groups decode to a Spanish word without a look-alike sign being settled by sense count as read; his own figure, sure plus probable, is given in brackets.

letterdate (1522)wordssurehis count
R96102 June1,137691 = 60.8%79.3%, 82.2% after a second pass
R961418 June1,258785 = 62.4%77.3%
R964022 October1,203702 = 58.4%71.5%
R96477 November1,072653 = 60.9%75.6%

All four are read in part. R9610 is the Viceroy of Naples’ 24,000 ducats for Prospero Colonna, the Signoria’s refusal of a safe-conduct through its lands and the escort from Verona instead, the King of France’s letters from Lyon, Lautrec leaving for France, the Turk’s ambassador at Venice taken for a spy and the Turk arming a hundred sails, and the Turks before Knin; Tomokiyo’s printed opening line agrees with the decode. R9640 and R9647 are new text: England’s detention of Venetian goods and the French ambassador’s threats, Geronimo Adorno’s plan for an enterprise against France with English money, Rhodes; and the Signoria’s answer to Sánchez and the English ambassador that it has no obligation left to France. The English files of these two are summaries, not translations. R9614 is not new text: its clear postscript says it is a copy sent by Genoa of the letter sent by post to England, and its text is that of R9613 (CSP Spain 2 no. 429), which this page already reads from its contemporary decipherment. Aligned word by word with that decipherment, 674 of its 785 sure words (85.9%) are the same, 694 with near spellings; the contributor says the decipherment was not used to settle any word. That is a check of the transcription and the key on a text whose plain text is known, and it holds. Of the unresolved words of R9610, 96 read only if a sign label of his transcription is corrected after decoding; a second, independent blind transcription confirmed 33 of them by a rule frozen before it was applied (control 3 of 94), and they count as probable, not sure.

The second test failed. The contributor proposed four values for code groups absent from the key (dud = vino, sod = govierno, lud = presente, cid = xxii) and fixed in advance one measure on a fresh passage, R9647 f. 257r (654 tokens, 1,185 characters), with 600 controls: the values were to be adopted only if their gain in the language score beat at least 190 of 200 random value sets. Only dud occurs there (twice), and its gain beats 75 of 200. Result: fail; nothing was added to the key, and the sign-label corrections were not adopted either. Re-run for this page with the same figures. On the same passage the key itself again scores far above the controls (−2.331 per character against a control mean of −2.930 and a best of −2.605, z = 6.18). Not checked here: every transcription against the DECODE images (the site is a login page for these records); the order of events and the second transcriber’s independence are by the contributor’s record.

07The letter alphabet, and a misidentified sign

Tomokiyo gives the substitution alphabet only as a hand-drawn table. Measured off the table at high zoom, it has about thirty signs over twenty-two letters (a b c d e f g h i l m n o p q r s t u x y z), most of them with two or three homophones: a = n, ʒf; d = ∞, 3, ɸ; g = bold ∞, L; n = ᶻ, ℞₄; and so on.

Two results follow. The “q with a double bar” is the letter s, and there is no separate plural marker. The plural ending is the letter s written after a code group, so Tomokiyo’s guess that it “may well be the plural ending -s” is right, for a different reason. And r is a different sign, a q with a loop, easily confused with it in this hand.

The map is carried in alpha.py and checks out on spelled runs taken from R9635 f. 20r: nx#gn = ahora, ttn#n = nada.

At this stage it did not yet give the spelled runs continuously, for the following reason. Tomokiyo transliterates the code groups with latin labels drawn from a set that includes v, which is not one of the twenty-two plaintext letters; the code labels and the alphabet therefore do not share one transliteration, and every sign has to be matched to both by eye. At the resolution DECODE serves, telling apart the homophone pairs Ɋ (r) and ǂ (s), 9 (l) and Ɋ (r), ∞ (d) and bold ∞ (g) is a judgement call at each occurrence, and getting it wrong silently produces plausible-looking Spanish that is not what the page says. So the runs are left marked rather than filled in.

The remedy is in the glosses: collate the patchy interlinear decipherment on R9635 f. 20r word by word against the signs beneath it, which fixes each homophone in this scribe’s hand; the unglossed lines and the rest of the group then follow. That is edition work against a published key, not cryptanalysis.

08R9605: the cipher aligned with the clerk’s decipherment

R9605 (9/24 f. 39–44, 7 May 1522) is the record from which Tomokiyo recovered the key, and its images show why: the cipher and the clerk’s full contemporary decipherment sit on facing leaves. Aligned at the end of the letter, where the clear reads Plegue a Dios no lo yerre en el camino. De Venecia a vij de mayo 1522:

cipherplaintexthow
nufnocode
paclocode
ɡɣβ4q4yerrespelled
vog vegen elcode — Tomokiyo’s own way in, met here in situ
zn·β·aɤ + nufcami + no = caminospelled, then a code group used for the last syllable

Two results follow that the key alone could not give. Code groups stand for syllables as well as whole words: nuf is no the adverb in one place and the final syllable of cami-no in the next, so a decryption that treats every group as a word will divide the text wrongly. And the lookalike signs are distinct: camino needs β = m, ahora (nx#Ɋn, R9635 f. 20r) needs Ɋ = r, and the bold ∞ is g. All three are a “g”-shape at the resolution DECODE serves, and they are the single largest source of error in the spelled runs.

Continuing the same passage against the clear “la venida del visorey con todas las postas, que despacho y escrivo” gives three more results. xel def = del visorey and ded+xof = veni+da = venida, verbatim. The spelled run for escrivo is seven signs for seven letters, fixing z = c, ɡ = r, aɤ = i, m = v, # = o with the known plaintext above them. And va = despacho, not “desponho”: Tomokiyo’s table gives a word that is not Spanish, where the clear reads que despacho y escrivo with ho = que and va in that slot. It also explains his puzzled “me desponho x-e-r-o-n que” in R9623. A correction to the published key.

One check came out inconclusive and is recorded as such. The clear at “que fue a …” stands over the group cud, which Tomokiyo gives as “xxiv?”; had the clear read xxv, the whole c-block series would shift by one and the values predicted here would be wrong. It does not settle the question: the date is written over a correction and reads as something like xxv y xxvij, two dates rather than one, so it cannot be matched to a single group. cab, cef and cif do not depend on it: each was confirmed in its own context, independently of where the run starts.

The same alignment runs from the very first word of the letter. Clear (f. 42): “Ya dixe a V. Mag. que el embaxador de Hungria fue respondido de los desta republica con buenas palabras…”; cipher (f. 39r ll. 1–2): cig = ya; vu·o·ɣ = di+x+e = dixe; dom pud ho veg = vuestra magestad que el; vam vuc·ɡ = embaxa+do+r = embaxador; n·℞·∞ ɡ·aɤ = Hun-gria, spelled across the line break; sa = fue; ɡ·ɣ·q·ao·#·& vu vuc = r-e-s-p-o-n + di + do = respondido; xig vum ɡ·ɣ lal = desta republica; xa yuc·n+s = con buenas.

None of this is conjecture: the clerk’s plaintext corresponds to every group on the facing leaf. It fixes the sign table against a known plaintext instead of by eye: n=a, ɣ=e, ɡ=r, aɤ=i, o=x, #=o, q=s, ao=p, &=n, m=v, z=c, β=m, and the q-with-double-bar = s. (q=s here against Tomokiyo’s alphabet column q=t: two similar glyphs, and the pair to watch next.)

Applied to R9635, which has only patchy glosses, the table alone does not decrypt it, which corrects the diagnosis above. On R9635 f. 20r the first line is ‖ como ‖ quien ‖ deg ho feb ʒnɡ xof+s yeh+s xig dom pud. The tail is secure (das cartas de vuestra magestad), and the null padding matches R9605 exactly. But deg, feb and ʒnɡ resolve to nothing: none is in the nomenclator, and the last spells y-a-r, which is not a word. Sense wants recebi in that slot, whose code is hug, unlike anything written there.

So the obstacle lies in the step before the sign table: turning the cursive shapes into labels. R9605’s hand and R9635’s differ enough that a shape read confidently as d in one is something else in the other, and a wrong label yields a code group that either does not exist or exists with the wrong value. The alignment has to be done on the image, glyph against the clerk’s word above it, building a reference set of each sign as this scribe forms it, instead of transcribing to labels first and resolving afterwards. That is slower and more reliable.

R9605 is therefore not counted as a result here: the Emperor’s clerk deciphered it in 1522, on the facing leaves. R9613, R9635 and R9641 were read on 23 October 2026 from contemporary decipherments of the same kind (see section 06) and are readings from an existing decipherment, not new decipherments. Transcribing a clear text gives a continuous plaintext of those letters, which is an edition of a 1522 decipherment. The records with no contemporary decipherment have no such facing plaintext.

The next step is to align the whole of R9605’s cipher against its decipherment, several hundred signs of known plaintext, to fix each homophone in this scribe’s hand, and then carry it to the unglossed pages.

09Transcription: how the page is written

The failure reported above came from a wrong assumption about how the page is written; correcting it made the page decipherable. Everything here is written in ordinary latin-looking cursive. The two systems are told apart by shape-length and context: three-letter latin trigrams are nomenclator codes (xig, vog, dom, pud, lig, nuc), looked up in Tomokiyo’s table; single letters and short runs between them are the substitution alphabet, where the letterform and the value differ: n=a, y=e, g=r, o/✗=x, #=o, ao=p, m=u/v, z=c, β=m, 9=l, q-with-double-bar = s. Three nulls open each paragraph, padded with the clear words como and quien.

Verified on R9605 f. 39r l. 1 against the clerk’s plaintext on the facing leaf: cig vu·o·y n dom pud ho veg = “Ya dixe a vuestra magestad que el”, with vu(di)+o(x)+y(e) = dixe.

With that, the page can be deciphered. R9635 f. 20r ll. 2–3 give continuous text:

lig vog vom nuf gad lel ao-y-g-xig-g nuc … xa pac+s xig vum g-y-lal
= “pues en esto no se puede perder nada … con los de esta republica”

Two things in that sentence are new. lel = puede, a code Tomokiyo did not recover: it falls between lal publica and lol pudi, and the sense fixes it. And perder is written p-e-r + xig (de) + r: a code group used inside a word for a syllable, the same pattern R9605 showed with cami + nuf(no) = camino.

Line 9 gives an independent check: nul (obliga) + vuc (do) + s = obligados, the word the 1522 decipherer wrote in the gloss above that stretch. Key, alphabet and gloss agree without being fitted to each other.

Further down the page: pef pac ho zel xig a-h-o | r-a = me lo que antes de ahora; yig (capitula) + c-i-o-n = capitulacion; and ho hob hec ho pac+s = que quien Inglaterra que los, with hec first read as Inglaterra; the contemporary decipherment found on 23 October 2026 gives quiera there (quien quiera que), so the Inglaterra reading is withdrawn (section 06).

Lower on the page: vom+s cad pol-9 xig a-h-o-r-a = estos xx mil de ahora; ao-# xig g pac suf-z-e-r lu-ho = poderlo hazer, porque; and rug+s lul ta = las quales. The last examples show the converse of the stem-plus-ending pattern, codes run together to make one word: lu(por)+ho(que) = porque, lul(qual)+ta(es) = quales, suf(ha)+z-e-r = hazer.

The page closes with pagarian todos los (first read as darian; corrected from the f. 22r decipherment on 23 October 2026) …, no pude …, si no se … en …, and then opens a fresh paragraph with the same three nulls and pac+s xig vum r-e-lal = los de esta republica. One code there, cag, is unassigned in Tomokiyo’s table and sits at the end of the numeral run immediately before cig (ya); cag = xxx is the natural continuation and suits the sense, but it is one slot past where the run is attested and is left probable, not certain.

Line 5 gives xof veg + p-r-i-m-e-r-o + xig ta = da el primero de es[te], which fixes β = m on this page independently of camino in R9605; and at high zoom the ending of capitulacion is the curly second form of c that Tomokiyo draws as a backwards 3, so that word is settled rather than inferred.

Every line of f. 20r now has at least a partial decipherment. Continuous or substantial: ll. 2–6, 9, 10, 11, 13, 15, 17, 18, 20, 22, 23, 25, with the passages at ll. 7 and 14; partial, with runs still unresolved: ll. 1, 8, 12, 16, 19, 21, 24. The subject, the sums, the parties and the drift of the argument are deciphered throughout the page, while a scatter of spelled runs inside partially deciphered lines stays open. It is not a word-perfect diplomatic edition and is not offered as one. (The crops were cut by dividing the written area into twenty-five equal bands, so the line numbers are approximate; each decipherment is tied to its own words.) The remaining runs need the same transcription work and no cryptanalysis; they are left untranscribed rather than filled in by inference. One token resists: gta, a dozen occurrences, cannot be a code (its middle position is not a vowel) and is most likely g (y) + ta (es), but that does not sit well everywhere and is left unresolved. The edition is in targets/sanchez1522/edition/.

Folio 21 is two further full pages of cipher, denser than f. 20r, with the decipherer’s gloss obligados … fuessen restituydas sus tierras continuing across it. One paragraph on the right-hand leaf, marked A in the margin, is written entirely in the clear, and it names the English ambassador:

M[icer] Ricardo Paceo, embax[ad]or de Inglaterra, me ha dado el incluso memorial y rogado que escriva a v[uest]ra m[agesta]d suplicandole por las cartas que dize para el Papa y para don Joan. Sup[li]co a v[uest]ra m[agesta]d muy humilmente, con primero las mande enbiar, pues es persona que merece toda buena obra de v[uest]ra m[agesta]d, por ser muy servidor suyo y aficionado &c.

Richard Pace, Henry VIII’s envoy at Venice, has given Sánchez an enclosed memorial and asked him to press the Emperor for the letters he wants for the Pope and for don Juan, that is Juan Manuel, the imperial ambassador at Rome, whose own 1522 cipher Tomokiyo reconstructed in the same article. Sánchez seconds the request warmly. The token hec on f. 20r, first read as Inglaterra, is quiera in the contemporary decipherment, so this identification does not rest on it; the English ambassador is the same Pace who opens R9631 in Tomokiyo’s list. The two leaves of f. 21 are not yet transcribed line by line.

10Lope Hurtado de Mendoza writes in a third cipher

The nine Lope Hurtado records bound in the same volumes look at first like more of the same: the same period, the same recipient, cipher mixed with clear inside the sentence, code groups of the same shape. They are not in Sánchez’s cipher.

The test is the third position. Sánchez’s codes end strictly in b c d f g h l m n. Folio 260v of R9648 gives tep, tap, and groups ending in z, finals that Sánchez’s key does not contain. Juan Manuel’s Rome cipher, by contrast, does use p r s t z as finals (jap papa, jir, jus, boz, qat). So Lope Hurtado’s key is a third member of the family.

Whether it is Juan Manuel’s own key is not settled here. None of the groups legible on that leaf (ton, tep, tap, xul, xild, zar, xurzun) has a value in Tomokiyo’s Juan Manuel table either, but that table is itself a partial recovery, so their absence decides nothing. Several of these records carry contemporary decipherments, so the key is recoverable by the same route Tomokiyo took; they need a separate target.

11What is open, and how reliable the decipherment is

  • 43 records not read. Twenty-two of them (lot 1: R9594, R9616, R9617, R9626, R9631, R9632, R9633, R9636, R9639, R9651; lot 2: R9603, R9604, R9608, R9609, R9611, R9612, R9618, R9620, R9623, R9624, R9628, R9630) were triaged on 12 November 2026, not read; the other Sánchez records of Salazar 9/23–9/26 (R9594 onward in date order; R9626/R9627 and R9628/R9629 are duplicates, and R9625 and R9638 are mostly clear) have not been worked. Only R9613, R9635 and R9641 (and R9605) were found with a contemporary decipherment on DECODE; the others are cipher-only there, so the work is a sign-by-sign reading with the published key. The target stays in progress until they are done.
  • R9653: decoded, not read; the second paragraph of its cipher (f. 299v ll. 7–12) and single groups of the first, on code groups absent from the published key; its second copy (f. 300v) and R9654 need the originals.
  • R9593: decoded, not read; the end of f. 27r (ll. 26–33), f. 27v ll. 11–13 and about twenty open code groups.
  • Uncertain words of the decipherments: four in R9635, three in R9613, two in R9641, in fast or cramped hands at DECODE’s resolution.
  • Lope Hurtado’s key is unreconstructed.
  • Grades. The resolved run of R9653’s foot paragraph is graded C: certain on the code groups, which are unambiguous against the published key and make continuous sense. The remainder of that paragraph and the whole head paragraph are I, incomplete. The clear middle is a transcription of ordinary Spanish secretary hand and carries no cipher grade; two words in it are conjectural and marked with brackets above.
  • cof xxviii, cuf xxix, cab xv, ceb xvi, cib xvii are predicted, not observed, and are flagged as such in the key file.

Full texts

The transcription and the decryption of every document on this page, so that the reading can be used and checked. Short files are shown here; all are in the repository.

R9635, Venice, 16 Sept 1522 (RAH Salazar 9/26 ff. 20-22)

Alonso Sánchez, imperial ambassador at Venice, to Charles V: the money owed by Venice under the treaty ('mas de xv mil ducados', 'allende de los otros xviii mil'), restitution of goods, 'los de esta republica' (calendared by Bergenroth, CSP Spain 2 no. 476). Edited against Tomokiyo's published key; only ll. 1-3, 6, 7, 14, 22 of f. 20r and the opening of f. 21 are given, cipher groups and reading side by side.

r9635_f20r.md

# Edition: R9635 f. 20r — Sánchez to Charles V, Venice, 16 September 1522

RAH Salazar 9/26 f. 20. Edition against Tomokiyo's published key (Cryptiana, 6 Sept 2025), with the
sign values calibrated on R9605's cipher and its 1522 decipherment (`../calibration_r9605.md`).
**This is edition work against a published key, not cryptanalysis.**

## Method, corrected

The earlier failure in this folder came from a wrong model of the writing. The page is not written in
exotic symbols: **everything is written in ordinary latin-looking cursive**, and the two systems are
told apart by shape-length and context —

- **three-letter latin trigrams are nomenclator codes** (`xig`, `vog`, `dom`, `pud`, `lig`, `nuc` …),
  read straight off Tomokiyo's table;
- **single letters and short runs between them are the substitution alphabet**, where the letterform
  and the plaintext value differ: `n`=a, `y`=e, `g`=r, `o`/`✗`=x, `#`=o, `ao`=p, `m`=u/v, `z`=c,
  `β`=m, `q`-with-double-bar = s, `9`=l;
- the three nulls `ℋ`, `℈#`, `ℍO` open each paragraph, padded with the meaningless clear words
  *como* and *quien*.

Confirmed on R9605 f. 39r l. 1, where the clerk's plaintext stands on the facing leaf:
`cig vu·o·y n dom pud ho veg` = **"Ya dixe a vuestra magestad que el"**, with `vu`(di)+`o`(x)+`y`(e)
= *dixe*.

## Lines read continuously

Codes in `code`, spelled letters hyphenated, *italic* = written in the clear on the page.

**l. 1** ‖ *como* ‖ *quien* ‖ `deg` `ho` `feb` `ʒong` `xof`+s `yeh`+s `xig` `dom` `pud`

> … que … **das cartas de vuestra magestad**

`deg`, `feb` and `ʒong` are codes Tomokiyo never recovered; bracketed, `deg` falls in the d-block
between *visorey de Napoles* and *vimes*, `feb` in the f-block between *tene* and *tanta*.

**ll. 2–3** `lig` `vog` `vom` `nuf` `gad` `lel` ao-y-g-`xig`-g `nuc` `Pmg` `xa` `pac`+s `xig` `vum`
g-y-`lal`

> **pues en esto no se puede perder nada … con los de esta republica**

- **`lel` = *puede*** — a new code value. It brackets in the l-block between `lal` *publica* and
  `lol` *pudi*, and the sense fixes it.
- *perder* is written p-e-r + `xig` (*de*) + r — a code group used mid-word for a syllable, the
  pattern R9605 showed with *cami*+`nuf`(*no*) = *camino*.

**l. 3** … `g` `vu`-✗-`y` … `ho` `cig`

> … y **dixe** … que **ya** …

`vu`-✗-`y` = *dixe* again, here with `✗` for x where R9605 used `o`: homophones of the same letter.

**l. 6** (the decipherer glosses *obligados* above this stretch)
`vud`+s `zef` `xig` `pac`+s `mud`+s `cob` pol-9

> **ducados allende de los otros xviii mil**

**l. 7** carries the gloss *fuessen restituydas sus bienes*.

**l. 14** `ped` `xig` `cab` pol-9 `vud`+s

> **mas de xv mil ducados**

**l. 22** `pac`+s `xig` `vum` g-y-`lal`

> **los de esta republica**

Also on the page: `yig` = *capitula* (l. 3), `gub` = *señor*, `xel def` = *del visorey*,
`faf`+s `pac`+s = *todos los*, `nef` = *ni*, `geg` = *si*, `nuf` = *no*.

**l. 4** … `ho` `xa` `dom` `pud` `fod`+s `zap`

> … que con **vuestra magestad** …

**l. 6 (right)** … `zef` `xig` `pac`+s `mud`+s `cob` pol-9 — *allende de los otros xviii mil*, the
reading already given, here confirmed on the right-hand half of the line.

**l. 9** `nul` `vuc`+s `gta` ao-#-& `vu`-y `g`-#

> **obligados** …

`nul` (*obliga*) + `vuc` (*do*) + s = **obligados** — and *obligados* is precisely the word the 1522
decipherer wrote in the gloss above this stretch. Key, alphabet and gloss agree independently. It is
the third instance on this page of a code group carrying a word-stem with the ending spelled or coded
after it.

**l. 10** g-n `ho` `gta` `sa` `Lo` `vog` `hag` `xof` …

> … que … **fue** … **en** …

with `Lo` = the letter t, one of the two forms Tomokiyo's table gives for it.

**l. 9 (right)** … `pef` `pac` `ho` `zel` `xig` n-x-# | g-n …

> … **me lo que antes de ahora** …

*ahora* is spelled a-h-o across the line break and finished r-a on the next — the same word that first
showed the sign `g` = r.

**l. 11** … `voc` `yig` z-aɥ-#-& | `gɣ` ao-g-aɥ …

> … **capitulacion** …

`yig` (*capitula*) + c-i-o-n. A fourth instance of the stem-plus-ending pattern.

**l. 13** … g-#-& `ho` `hob` `hec` `ho` `pac`+s

> … -ron **que quien Inglaterra que los** …

`hec` = *Inglaterra*. The English ambassador at Venice is the running subject of these months —
R9621, which Tomokiyo prints entire, turns on the Signoria's wish for his arrival.

**l. 17** `vom`+s `cad` pol-9 `xig` n-x-#-g-n

> **estos xx mil de ahora**

**l. 18** ao-# `xig` g `pac` `suf`-A-ɣ-g `lu`-`ho`

> **poderlo hazer, porque** …

Two nice compounds: `suf` (*ha*) + z-e-r = **hazer**, and `lu` (*por*) + `ho` (*que*) = **porque** —
two codes run together to make one word, the mirror of the stem-plus-ending pattern.

**l. 19** `vud`+s … `gam` … `mod`

> **ducados** … su … **os** …

**l. 20** … `rug`+s `lul` `ta` … 

> … **las quales** …

`lul` (*qual*) + `ta` (*es*) — codes run together again.

**l. 21** `xof` n+s g-#-# `ho` `vog` `hag` `vog` …

> … que **en** … **en** …

**l. 22** ∞-n-g-aɥ-n+s `faf`+s `pac`+s `cag` …

> **darian todos los** … 

`cag` is unassigned in Tomokiyo's table. It sits in the c-block at C3=g, immediately before `cig`
(*ya*) — that is, at the end of the numeral run and the start of the *y*- words. Given `caf` = xxv and
`cuf` = xxix, **`cag` = xxx** is the natural continuation and suits the sense (sums in thousands of
ducats run through this whole page), but it is one slot past where the run is attested and is left as
probable, not certain.

**l. 23** `nuf` ao-m `xig` g-n-z-n-g `pac`+s …

> **no pude** de … **los** …

**l. 24** … `geg` `nuf` `gad` `gta` `hag` `vog` …

> … **si no se** … **en** …

**l. 25** ‖ ‖ ‖ `pac`+s `xig` `vum` g-y-`lal` …

> **los de esta republica** …

The page ends by opening a fresh paragraph with the same three nulls and the phrase these despatches
turn on.

**l. 5** `xof` `veg` ao-g-aɥ-β-y-g-# `xig` `ta` …

> **da el primero de es**[te] …

*primero* spelled p-r-i-m-e-r-o. This is what fixes `β` = **m** on this page independently of
*camino* in R9605.

**l. 11, at high zoom** — the ending of *capitulacion* is ∂-aɥ-#-&, where `∂` is the curly second
form of **c** that Tomokiyo draws as `Ә` in his alphabet. So `yig` + c-i-o-n = *capitulacion* is
settled, not inferred.

**l. 15** `cob` pol-9 `vud`+s ao-g-aɥ-β-y-g …

> **xviii mil ducados primer**[o] …

*primero* again, spelled the same way, in the same money context as l. 6.

**l. 13** … `dom` `pud` `sam` …

> … **vuestra magestad** … 

**l. 16** `hag` `xof`+s `rug`+s `voc` …

> … **das las** … 

## A caveat on the line numbers

The crops were cut by dividing the written area into twenty-five equal bands, so the line numbers
above are approximate and may be out by one where the scribe's spacing drifts. The *readings* do not
depend on them — each is anchored to its own words — but anyone collating this against the manuscript
should locate the phrases, not the numbers.

### A recurring token still open

`gta` occurs a dozen times on this page and is not in the nomenclator; it cannot be a code, since its
middle position is not a vowel. It is most likely `g` (the conjunction *y*) followed by the two-letter
code `ta` (*es*), giving *y es*, but that does not sit well in every occurrence and it is left
unresolved rather than forced.

## What the letter is about

The sums owed by the Signoria under the capitulation — eighteen thousand ducats outstanding from a
previous year, more than fifteen thousand in another reckoning — and the restitution of goods seized
from imperial vassals, which the decipherer glossed *fuessen restituydas sus bienes*. The same
business fills R9638 (11 October 1522) entirely in clear, and is the reckoning Tomokiyo read in the
last paragraph of R9623.

## Coverage

Every line of f. 20r now has a reading of some kind. Continuous or substantial: ll. 2–6, 9, 10, 11,
13, 15, 17, 18, 20, 22, 23, 25 and the passages at ll. 7, 14. Partial, with runs still unresolved:
ll. 1, 8, 12, 16, 19, 21, 24.

The page is **read throughout in substance** — subject, sums, parties and the drift of the argument —
while a scatter of spelled runs inside partially-read lines stays open. It is not a word-perfect
diplomatic edition and is not offered as one.

The remaining lines are the same work, line by line, at the same zoom: nothing in them is
cryptanalytically novel, and the method above applies unchanged. They are left untranscribed rather
than filled in by inference.

## New to the key from this page

| code | value | how |
|---|---|---|
| `lel` | **puede** | bracketed between `lal` *publica* and `lol` *pudi*, fixed by sense in "no se puede perder nada" |
| — | *(pattern)* | codes run together to make one word: `lu`+`ho` = **porque**, `lul`+`ta` = **quales**, `suf`+z-e-r = **hazer** |
| `cab` | **xv** | attested in "mas de xv mil ducados" (predicted earlier from the ordering rule) |
| `cag` | **xxx** *(probable)* | sits at the end of the numeral run, immediately before `cig` *ya*; suits "todos los … mil" but is one slot past the attested run |
Transcription

r9635_f21.md

# Edition: R9635 f. 21 — Sánchez to Charles V, Venice, 16 September 1522

RAH Salazar 9/26 f. 21 (DECODE image `IMG_R9635_I45364_P2.jpg`). Edition against Tomokiyo's
published key; method and sign values as in `r9635_f20r.md` and `../calibration_r9605.md`.

## Shape of the opening

Both leaves are **full pages of cipher**, denser than f. 20r — roughly thirty lines each. Two things
interrupt the cipher on the right-hand leaf:

1. the decipherer's gloss **obligados … fuessen restituydas sus tierras** written between the lines
   near the head — the same gloss that runs across f. 20r, so the restitution business continues here;
2. a **paragraph written entirely in the clear**, marked in the margin with an `A`, about the English
   ambassador.

## The clear paragraph on Richard Pace

Read from the image (abbreviations expanded in brackets):

> M[icer] Ricardo Paceo, embax[ad]or de Inglaterra, me ha dado el incluso memorial y rogado que
> escriva a v[uest]ra m[agesta]d suplicandole por las cartas que dize para el Papa y para don Joan.
> Sup[li]co a v[uest]ra m[agesta]d muy humilmente, con primero las mande enbiar, pues es persona que
> merece toda buena obra de v[uest]ra m[agesta]d, por ser muy servidor suyo y aficionado &c.

**Richard Pace**, Henry VIII's envoy at Venice, has given Sánchez an enclosed memorial and asked him
to write to the Emperor on his behalf — pressing for the letters Pace wants for the Pope and for
**don Juan** (Juan Manuel, the imperial ambassador at Rome, whose own 1522 cipher Tomokiyo
reconstructed in the same article). Sánchez seconds the request warmly.

This fixes the identification behind the code `hec` = *Inglaterra* met on f. 20r, and it is the same
Pace who opens R9631 in Tomokiyo's list ("Mi-c-e-r [R]-i-c-a-r-do p-a-c-e-o embaxa-do-r del
se-r-e-ni-ss-i-[m]-o"). The English ambassador's arrival and doings are the thread running through
the Venetian despatches of these months; R9621, the one letter Tomokiyo prints entire, turns on the
Signoria's wish for him.

## Cipher on the leaf: what is legible at this pass

Codes read off without difficulty in the opening lines of the right-hand leaf include `xig` (*de*),
`nuf` (*no*), `zem` (*aqui*), `dal` (*un*), `vog` (*en*), `xel` (*del*), `ho` (*que*), `vum`
(*esta*), g-y-`lal` (*republica*), `gad` (*se*), `vuc` (*do*), `pac`+s (*los*), `ped` (*mas*),
`vam vuc`+g (*embaxador*), `gam` (*su*), `gub` (*señor*), `hac`/`hed` (*rey*), `mef` (*paz*),
`fob` (*tambien*), `sef` (*guarda*), `mod` (*os*), `yeh`+q (*cartas*), `dom pud` (*vuestra
magestad*), `A`-words and the nulls opening each paragraph.

**The two leaves are not yet transcribed line by line.** What is established here is their structure,
the continuation of the restitution matter, and the clear Pace paragraph in full.

## First two lines of the right-hand leaf

`xig`+s ʒ-o-y `dod` `nuf` `zem` `dal` β `vog` Lo n-aɥ-y-g-# `xel` g-n & aɥ-n
`ho` `xig` n-g-b-m `nef` ʒ-o `vec`+s `ho` … `pac` … `xig` `vum` …

> … **vi no aqui un** … **del** … / **que de** … **ni** … **dize** … **lo** … **de esta**
> [republica] …

The hand on f. 21 is smaller and more cursive than f. 20r and the spelled runs between the codes do
not yet resolve cleanly. The codes read without difficulty; the letters need the same picture-book
treatment given to f. 20r, on this scribe's forms. Recorded as fragments rather than smoothed into a
sentence.

## Open

Line-by-line transcription of both leaves of f. 21, and of f. 22 (`IMG_R9635_I45364_P3.jpg`).
Nothing here is cryptanalytically novel; it is time at the images.

R9635_reading.md

# R9635: Alonso Sánchez to Charles V, Venice, 16 September 1522 (RAH Salazar 9/26 ff. 20-22), reading

Re-read 23 Oct 2026 (Claude Opus 5.5, REREAD_BRIEF batch b23); log in `R9635_reread_log.md`. **Rests on the contemporary
decipherment of the letter, a clear copy in a court hand on f. 22r** (DECODE image `IMG_R9635_I45364_P3.jpg`, right
page; f. 21v, the left page of the same image, is the address leaf with the docket "Venecia a xvi de setiembre"
written upside down). It had not been noticed: the earlier work in this folder (`read_r9635.md`,
`edition/r9635_f20r.md`) knew only the patchy interlinear glosses on the cipher pages. The decipherment was checked
against the cipher figures of ff. 20r, 20v and 21r with Tomokiyo's key wherever the earlier edition or this pass had read
them (see Notes); every check agrees except `hec` (below).

Conventions: plain = carried by the decipherment (and, where noted, the figures); `[?word]` partly legible;
`[word]` supplied. The tear across f. 22r ll. 9-11 is filled from the cipher of f. 20r ll. 11-14, read sign by sign on
23 Oct 2026 (r2i): *capitulacion. Repliqueles … del señor infante, y respondieron que … tuviese* (Notes). Spelling as written; *v. mad.* and *vra. ma.* expanded to
*vuestra magestad*.

## Reading

Visto que [?ya] tanto tardavan cartas de vuestra magestad, pues en esto no se puede perder nada, fuy con los desta
republica y dixeles que ya sabian que por la capitulacion de la tregua que con vuestra magestad tienen assentada, el
primero deste mes havian de pagar otros xx mil ducados, allende de los otros xviij mil que deven del otro año; que
les rogava quisiessen pagarlos y cumplir con lo que son obligados. Respondieronme lo que antes de agora, que les
fuessen restituydas sus tierras segun esta assentado en la dicha capitulacion. Repliqueles que las tierras estavan en
poder del señor infante, y respondieron que quien quiera que los tuviese, vuestra magestad havia prometido
restituyrlas. Dixeles que les [?…] [?bastar] tener los
xviij mil ducados primeros hasta que fuessen restituydas las dichas tierras, y que pagassen estos xx mil de agora.
Replicaronme no poderlo hazer, porque ellos havian pagado y pagavan cada año mas de seys mil ducados a sus subditos
por las dichas tierras, las quales valian mas de xv mil ducados cada año; que en restituyendolas ellos pagarian todos
los xx mil ducados luego. De esto no pude sacarlos, ni creo jamas los pagaran si no se les restituyen las dichas
tierras.

Los desta republica han [?tenido] estos dias cartas de [?Menorami] de xxviij del passado de [?Lucerna], y escrive que
tenian todos los suyos que por agora yrian a servir en Francia a la parte de Picardia, y que por todo este mes no se
podria mas campear en aquella parte y vernan a la empresa de Italia. Tambien tienen estos cartas de su embaxador que
esta en Francia de xxvj del passado; dize que el rey de Francia yva de Bles a Paris y de alli a Picardia. Por dos vias
me han certificado esto.

## Notes

- **The decipherment against the figures.** Places where the cipher was read sign by sign (here or in
  `edition/r9635_f20r.md`) and the decipherment stands in the same order: *visto que … tanto* (`deg` `ho` `feb`, the
  last two unassigned in Tomokiyo, now *visto*, *tanto* by slot and this text); *cartas de vuestra magestad*; *pues en
  esto no se puede perder nada* (`lig vog vom nuf gad lel` p-e-r-`xig`-r `nuc`); *con los de esta republica*; *dixe(les)
  … que ya* (`vu`-x-e … `ho cig`); *capitulacion* (`yig`+c-i-o-n); *el primero deste mes* (`veg` p-r-i-m-e-r-o `xig ta`);
  *obligados* (`nul vuc`+s, under the 1522 gloss *obligados*); *allende de los otros xviii mil* (`zef xig pac`+s `mud`+s
  `cob pol`-l); *xviii mil ducados primeros*; *mas de xv mil ducados* (`ped xig cab pol`-l `vud`+s); *estos xx mil de
  agora* (`vom`+s `cad pol`-l `xig` a-h-o-r-a); *poderlo hazer, porque* (p-o `xig` r `pac` `suf`-z-e-r `lu`-`ho`); *las
  quales*; *todos los*; *no pude* (`nuf` p-u `xig`); *si no se*; on f. 20v *los suyos … en Francia la parte de*
  (`gim` = *suyos*: Tomokiyo's "suquos?") and *tambien … embaxador que esta en Francia … el rey de Francia … de alli …
  por dos … me*. 
- **Disagreements.** The earlier edition's *darian todos los* (f. 20r l. 22) is *pagarian todos los* here (the first
  sign was taken for `∞` d); `hec` = *Inglaterra* (f. 20r l. 13, "que quien Inglaterra que los") does not stand in the
  decipherment, whose text there is *en poder […] quien quiera q los*: r2i reads `hec` *quiera* (below). *ahora* of the
  cipher (a-h-o-r-a) is *agora* in the decipherment; the reading follows the decipherment's word, the figures give *ahora*.
- **The tear (r2i, 23 Oct 2026).** f. 22r ll. 9-11 lose about ten words. The passage stands in cipher on f. 20r ll.
  11-14 (DECODE image P1, right page), read sign by sign at 2.5x (`R9635_reread_log.md`, r2i): `vog rug vac yig`
  ∂-a#-#-& = *en la dicha capitulacion*; g-4-ao-g-a# `ho` g-`ta` = r-e-p-l-i + *que* + l + *es* = *repliqueles*;
  `ho` g-`ta` `ho rug`+s qa#4fng `vum`+v-a-n `vog` ao-#-`xig`-g = *que les que las tierras estavan en poder*; `xel gub
  rac` g g-`ta` ao-#-& `vu` 4-g-#-& `ho hob hec ho pac`+s = *del señor infante y respondieron que quien quiera que
  los*; ✗ₒ-m-g#-a#-4-Lo-∂ `dom pud` = *tuviese vuestra magestad*. The decipherment's surviving words (*…es que las
  tierras estavan en poder … quien quiera q los … vra. ma. havia prometido*) stand in the same order, so the gap is
  carried by the figures. Two key values follow: `hec` = **quiera** (h-block between `hob` quien and `hed` rey) and
  `vac` = **dicha** (v-block, before `vec` dize, `vic` dicho); and `gta`, the "recurring token" of the edition, is
  the l-sign + `ta` (es) = *les* (also *que les fue[ssen]*, l. 10). The earlier "-ron que quien Inglaterra que los"
  was this passage misread.
- *[?Menorami]* is written *me|norami* across a line break: a correspondent of the Signoria, not identified.
  *[?Lucerna]* reads *delureina* (one stroke short of *Lucerna*); the Swiss levies for Picardy fit it.
Decryption

r9635_f20r.md

# Edition: R9635 f. 20r — Sánchez to Charles V, Venice, 16 September 1522

RAH Salazar 9/26 f. 20. Edition against Tomokiyo's published key (Cryptiana, 6 Sept 2025), with the
sign values calibrated on R9605's cipher and its 1522 decipherment (`../calibration_r9605.md`).
**This is edition work against a published key, not cryptanalysis.**

## Method, corrected

The earlier failure in this folder came from a wrong model of the writing. The page is not written in
exotic symbols: **everything is written in ordinary latin-looking cursive**, and the two systems are
told apart by shape-length and context —

- **three-letter latin trigrams are nomenclator codes** (`xig`, `vog`, `dom`, `pud`, `lig`, `nuc` …),
  read straight off Tomokiyo's table;
- **single letters and short runs between them are the substitution alphabet**, where the letterform
  and the plaintext value differ: `n`=a, `y`=e, `g`=r, `o`/`✗`=x, `#`=o, `ao`=p, `m`=u/v, `z`=c,
  `β`=m, `q`-with-double-bar = s, `9`=l;
- the three nulls `ℋ`, `℈#`, `ℍO` open each paragraph, padded with the meaningless clear words
  *como* and *quien*.

Confirmed on R9605 f. 39r l. 1, where the clerk's plaintext stands on the facing leaf:
`cig vu·o·y n dom pud ho veg` = **"Ya dixe a vuestra magestad que el"**, with `vu`(di)+`o`(x)+`y`(e)
= *dixe*.

## Lines read continuously

Codes in `code`, spelled letters hyphenated, *italic* = written in the clear on the page.

**l. 1** ‖ *como* ‖ *quien* ‖ `deg` `ho` `feb` `ʒong` `xof`+s `yeh`+s `xig` `dom` `pud`

> … que … **das cartas de vuestra magestad**

`deg`, `feb` and `ʒong` are codes Tomokiyo never recovered; bracketed, `deg` falls in the d-block
between *visorey de Napoles* and *vimes*, `feb` in the f-block between *tene* and *tanta*.

**ll. 2–3** `lig` `vog` `vom` `nuf` `gad` `lel` ao-y-g-`xig`-g `nuc` `Pmg` `xa` `pac`+s `xig` `vum`
g-y-`lal`

> **pues en esto no se puede perder nada … con los de esta republica**

- **`lel` = *puede*** — a new code value. It brackets in the l-block between `lal` *publica* and
  `lol` *pudi*, and the sense fixes it.
- *perder* is written p-e-r + `xig` (*de*) + r — a code group used mid-word for a syllable, the
  pattern R9605 showed with *cami*+`nuf`(*no*) = *camino*.

**l. 3** … `g` `vu`-✗-`y` … `ho` `cig`

> … y **dixe** … que **ya** …

`vu`-✗-`y` = *dixe* again, here with `✗` for x where R9605 used `o`: homophones of the same letter.

**l. 6** (the decipherer glosses *obligados* above this stretch)
`vud`+s `zef` `xig` `pac`+s `mud`+s `cob` pol-9

> **ducados allende de los otros xviii mil**

**l. 7** carries the gloss *fuessen restituydas sus bienes*.

**l. 14** `ped` `xig` `cab` pol-9 `vud`+s

> **mas de xv mil ducados**

**l. 22** `pac`+s `xig` `vum` g-y-`lal`

> **los de esta republica**

Also on the page: `yig` = *capitula* (l. 3), `gub` = *señor*, `xel def` = *del visorey*,
`faf`+s `pac`+s = *todos los*, `nef` = *ni*, `geg` = *si*, `nuf` = *no*.

**l. 4** … `ho` `xa` `dom` `pud` `fod`+s `zap`

> … que con **vuestra magestad** …

**l. 6 (right)** … `zef` `xig` `pac`+s `mud`+s `cob` pol-9 — *allende de los otros xviii mil*, the
reading already given, here confirmed on the right-hand half of the line.

**l. 9** `nul` `vuc`+s `gta` ao-#-& `vu`-y `g`-#

> **obligados** …

`nul` (*obliga*) + `vuc` (*do*) + s = **obligados** — and *obligados* is precisely the word the 1522
decipherer wrote in the gloss above this stretch. Key, alphabet and gloss agree independently. It is
the third instance on this page of a code group carrying a word-stem with the ending spelled or coded
after it.

**l. 10** g-n `ho` `gta` `sa` `Lo` `vog` `hag` `xof` …

> … que … **fue** … **en** …

with `Lo` = the letter t, one of the two forms Tomokiyo's table gives for it.

**l. 9 (right)** … `pef` `pac` `ho` `zel` `xig` n-x-# | g-n …

> … **me lo que antes de ahora** …

*ahora* is spelled a-h-o across the line break and finished r-a on the next — the same word that first
showed the sign `g` = r.

**l. 11** … `voc` `yig` z-aɥ-#-& | `gɣ` ao-g-aɥ …

> … **capitulacion** …

`yig` (*capitula*) + c-i-o-n. A fourth instance of the stem-plus-ending pattern.

**l. 13** … g-#-& `ho` `hob` `hec` `ho` `pac`+s

> … -ron **que quien Inglaterra que los** …

`hec` = *Inglaterra*. The English ambassador at Venice is the running subject of these months —
R9621, which Tomokiyo prints entire, turns on the Signoria's wish for his arrival.

**l. 17** `vom`+s `cad` pol-9 `xig` n-x-#-g-n

> **estos xx mil de ahora**

**l. 18** ao-# `xig` g `pac` `suf`-A-ɣ-g `lu`-`ho`

> **poderlo hazer, porque** …

Two nice compounds: `suf` (*ha*) + z-e-r = **hazer**, and `lu` (*por*) + `ho` (*que*) = **porque** —
two codes run together to make one word, the mirror of the stem-plus-ending pattern.

**l. 19** `vud`+s … `gam` … `mod`

> **ducados** … su … **os** …

**l. 20** … `rug`+s `lul` `ta` … 

> … **las quales** …

`lul` (*qual*) + `ta` (*es*) — codes run together again.

**l. 21** `xof` n+s g-#-# `ho` `vog` `hag` `vog` …

> … que **en** … **en** …

**l. 22** ∞-n-g-aɥ-n+s `faf`+s `pac`+s `cag` …

> **darian todos los** … 

`cag` is unassigned in Tomokiyo's table. It sits in the c-block at C3=g, immediately before `cig`
(*ya*) — that is, at the end of the numeral run and the start of the *y*- words. Given `caf` = xxv and
`cuf` = xxix, **`cag` = xxx** is the natural continuation and suits the sense (sums in thousands of
ducats run through this whole page), but it is one slot past where the run is attested and is left as
probable, not certain.

**l. 23** `nuf` ao-m `xig` g-n-z-n-g `pac`+s …

> **no pude** de … **los** …

**l. 24** … `geg` `nuf` `gad` `gta` `hag` `vog` …

> … **si no se** … **en** …

**l. 25** ‖ ‖ ‖ `pac`+s `xig` `vum` g-y-`lal` …

> **los de esta republica** …

The page ends by opening a fresh paragraph with the same three nulls and the phrase these despatches
turn on.

**l. 5** `xof` `veg` ao-g-aɥ-β-y-g-# `xig` `ta` …

> **da el primero de es**[te] …

*primero* spelled p-r-i-m-e-r-o. This is what fixes `β` = **m** on this page independently of
*camino* in R9605.

**l. 11, at high zoom** — the ending of *capitulacion* is ∂-aɥ-#-&, where `∂` is the curly second
form of **c** that Tomokiyo draws as `Ә` in his alphabet. So `yig` + c-i-o-n = *capitulacion* is
settled, not inferred.

**l. 15** `cob` pol-9 `vud`+s ao-g-aɥ-β-y-g …

> **xviii mil ducados primer**[o] …

*primero* again, spelled the same way, in the same money context as l. 6.

**l. 13** … `dom` `pud` `sam` …

> … **vuestra magestad** … 

**l. 16** `hag` `xof`+s `rug`+s `voc` …

> … **das las** … 

## A caveat on the line numbers

The crops were cut by dividing the written area into twenty-five equal bands, so the line numbers
above are approximate and may be out by one where the scribe's spacing drifts. The *readings* do not
depend on them — each is anchored to its own words — but anyone collating this against the manuscript
should locate the phrases, not the numbers.

### A recurring token still open

`gta` occurs a dozen times on this page and is not in the nomenclator; it cannot be a code, since its
middle position is not a vowel. It is most likely `g` (the conjunction *y*) followed by the two-letter
code `ta` (*es*), giving *y es*, but that does not sit well in every occurrence and it is left
unresolved rather than forced.

## What the letter is about

The sums owed by the Signoria under the capitulation — eighteen thousand ducats outstanding from a
previous year, more than fifteen thousand in another reckoning — and the restitution of goods seized
from imperial vassals, which the decipherer glossed *fuessen restituydas sus bienes*. The same
business fills R9638 (11 October 1522) entirely in clear, and is the reckoning Tomokiyo read in the
last paragraph of R9623.

## Coverage

Every line of f. 20r now has a reading of some kind. Continuous or substantial: ll. 2–6, 9, 10, 11,
13, 15, 17, 18, 20, 22, 23, 25 and the passages at ll. 7, 14. Partial, with runs still unresolved:
ll. 1, 8, 12, 16, 19, 21, 24.

The page is **read throughout in substance** — subject, sums, parties and the drift of the argument —
while a scatter of spelled runs inside partially-read lines stays open. It is not a word-perfect
diplomatic edition and is not offered as one.

The remaining lines are the same work, line by line, at the same zoom: nothing in them is
cryptanalytically novel, and the method above applies unchanged. They are left untranscribed rather
than filled in by inference.

## New to the key from this page

| code | value | how |
|---|---|---|
| `lel` | **puede** | bracketed between `lal` *publica* and `lol` *pudi*, fixed by sense in "no se puede perder nada" |
| — | *(pattern)* | codes run together to make one word: `lu`+`ho` = **porque**, `lul`+`ta` = **quales**, `suf`+z-e-r = **hazer** |
| `cab` | **xv** | attested in "mas de xv mil ducados" (predicted earlier from the ordering rule) |
| `cag` | **xxx** *(probable)* | sits at the end of the numeral run, immediately before `cig` *ya*; suits "todos los … mil" but is one slot past the attested run |
Decryption

r9635_f21.md

# Edition: R9635 f. 21 — Sánchez to Charles V, Venice, 16 September 1522

RAH Salazar 9/26 f. 21 (DECODE image `IMG_R9635_I45364_P2.jpg`). Edition against Tomokiyo's
published key; method and sign values as in `r9635_f20r.md` and `../calibration_r9605.md`.

## Shape of the opening

Both leaves are **full pages of cipher**, denser than f. 20r — roughly thirty lines each. Two things
interrupt the cipher on the right-hand leaf:

1. the decipherer's gloss **obligados … fuessen restituydas sus tierras** written between the lines
   near the head — the same gloss that runs across f. 20r, so the restitution business continues here;
2. a **paragraph written entirely in the clear**, marked in the margin with an `A`, about the English
   ambassador.

## The clear paragraph on Richard Pace

Read from the image (abbreviations expanded in brackets):

> M[icer] Ricardo Paceo, embax[ad]or de Inglaterra, me ha dado el incluso memorial y rogado que
> escriva a v[uest]ra m[agesta]d suplicandole por las cartas que dize para el Papa y para don Joan.
> Sup[li]co a v[uest]ra m[agesta]d muy humilmente, con primero las mande enbiar, pues es persona que
> merece toda buena obra de v[uest]ra m[agesta]d, por ser muy servidor suyo y aficionado &c.

**Richard Pace**, Henry VIII's envoy at Venice, has given Sánchez an enclosed memorial and asked him
to write to the Emperor on his behalf — pressing for the letters Pace wants for the Pope and for
**don Juan** (Juan Manuel, the imperial ambassador at Rome, whose own 1522 cipher Tomokiyo
reconstructed in the same article). Sánchez seconds the request warmly.

This fixes the identification behind the code `hec` = *Inglaterra* met on f. 20r, and it is the same
Pace who opens R9631 in Tomokiyo's list ("Mi-c-e-r [R]-i-c-a-r-do p-a-c-e-o embaxa-do-r del
se-r-e-ni-ss-i-[m]-o"). The English ambassador's arrival and doings are the thread running through
the Venetian despatches of these months; R9621, the one letter Tomokiyo prints entire, turns on the
Signoria's wish for him.

## Cipher on the leaf: what is legible at this pass

Codes read off without difficulty in the opening lines of the right-hand leaf include `xig` (*de*),
`nuf` (*no*), `zem` (*aqui*), `dal` (*un*), `vog` (*en*), `xel` (*del*), `ho` (*que*), `vum`
(*esta*), g-y-`lal` (*republica*), `gad` (*se*), `vuc` (*do*), `pac`+s (*los*), `ped` (*mas*),
`vam vuc`+g (*embaxador*), `gam` (*su*), `gub` (*señor*), `hac`/`hed` (*rey*), `mef` (*paz*),
`fob` (*tambien*), `sef` (*guarda*), `mod` (*os*), `yeh`+q (*cartas*), `dom pud` (*vuestra
magestad*), `A`-words and the nulls opening each paragraph.

**The two leaves are not yet transcribed line by line.** What is established here is their structure,
the continuation of the restitution matter, and the clear Pace paragraph in full.

## First two lines of the right-hand leaf

`xig`+s ʒ-o-y `dod` `nuf` `zem` `dal` β `vog` Lo n-aɥ-y-g-# `xel` g-n & aɥ-n
`ho` `xig` n-g-b-m `nef` ʒ-o `vec`+s `ho` … `pac` … `xig` `vum` …

> … **vi no aqui un** … **del** … / **que de** … **ni** … **dize** … **lo** … **de esta**
> [republica] …

The hand on f. 21 is smaller and more cursive than f. 20r and the spelled runs between the codes do
not yet resolve cleanly. The codes read without difficulty; the letters need the same picture-book
treatment given to f. 20r, on this scribe's forms. Recorded as fragments rather than smoothed into a
sentence.

## Open

Line-by-line transcription of both leaves of f. 21, and of f. 22 (`IMG_R9635_I45364_P3.jpg`).
Nothing here is cryptanalytically novel; it is time at the images.
Reading

R9635_reading.md

# R9635: Alonso Sánchez to Charles V, Venice, 16 September 1522 (RAH Salazar 9/26 ff. 20-22), reading

Re-read 23 Oct 2026 (Claude Opus 5.5, REREAD_BRIEF batch b23); log in `R9635_reread_log.md`. **Rests on the contemporary
decipherment of the letter, a clear copy in a court hand on f. 22r** (DECODE image `IMG_R9635_I45364_P3.jpg`, right
page; f. 21v, the left page of the same image, is the address leaf with the docket "Venecia a xvi de setiembre"
written upside down). It had not been noticed: the earlier work in this folder (`read_r9635.md`,
`edition/r9635_f20r.md`) knew only the patchy interlinear glosses on the cipher pages. The decipherment was checked
against the cipher figures of ff. 20r, 20v and 21r with Tomokiyo's key wherever the earlier edition or this pass had read
them (see Notes); every check agrees except `hec` (below).

Conventions: plain = carried by the decipherment (and, where noted, the figures); `[?word]` partly legible;
`[word]` supplied. The tear across f. 22r ll. 9-11 is filled from the cipher of f. 20r ll. 11-14, read sign by sign on
23 Oct 2026 (r2i): *capitulacion. Repliqueles … del señor infante, y respondieron que … tuviese* (Notes). Spelling as written; *v. mad.* and *vra. ma.* expanded to
*vuestra magestad*.

## Reading

Visto que [?ya] tanto tardavan cartas de vuestra magestad, pues en esto no se puede perder nada, fuy con los desta
republica y dixeles que ya sabian que por la capitulacion de la tregua que con vuestra magestad tienen assentada, el
primero deste mes havian de pagar otros xx mil ducados, allende de los otros xviij mil que deven del otro año; que
les rogava quisiessen pagarlos y cumplir con lo que son obligados. Respondieronme lo que antes de agora, que les
fuessen restituydas sus tierras segun esta assentado en la dicha capitulacion. Repliqueles que las tierras estavan en
poder del señor infante, y respondieron que quien quiera que los tuviese, vuestra magestad havia prometido
restituyrlas. Dixeles que les [?…] [?bastar] tener los
xviij mil ducados primeros hasta que fuessen restituydas las dichas tierras, y que pagassen estos xx mil de agora.
Replicaronme no poderlo hazer, porque ellos havian pagado y pagavan cada año mas de seys mil ducados a sus subditos
por las dichas tierras, las quales valian mas de xv mil ducados cada año; que en restituyendolas ellos pagarian todos
los xx mil ducados luego. De esto no pude sacarlos, ni creo jamas los pagaran si no se les restituyen las dichas
tierras.

Los desta republica han [?tenido] estos dias cartas de [?Menorami] de xxviij del passado de [?Lucerna], y escrive que
tenian todos los suyos que por agora yrian a servir en Francia a la parte de Picardia, y que por todo este mes no se
podria mas campear en aquella parte y vernan a la empresa de Italia. Tambien tienen estos cartas de su embaxador que
esta en Francia de xxvj del passado; dize que el rey de Francia yva de Bles a Paris y de alli a Picardia. Por dos vias
me han certificado esto.

## Notes

- **The decipherment against the figures.** Places where the cipher was read sign by sign (here or in
  `edition/r9635_f20r.md`) and the decipherment stands in the same order: *visto que … tanto* (`deg` `ho` `feb`, the
  last two unassigned in Tomokiyo, now *visto*, *tanto* by slot and this text); *cartas de vuestra magestad*; *pues en
  esto no se puede perder nada* (`lig vog vom nuf gad lel` p-e-r-`xig`-r `nuc`); *con los de esta republica*; *dixe(les)
  … que ya* (`vu`-x-e … `ho cig`); *capitulacion* (`yig`+c-i-o-n); *el primero deste mes* (`veg` p-r-i-m-e-r-o `xig ta`);
  *obligados* (`nul vuc`+s, under the 1522 gloss *obligados*); *allende de los otros xviii mil* (`zef xig pac`+s `mud`+s
  `cob pol`-l); *xviii mil ducados primeros*; *mas de xv mil ducados* (`ped xig cab pol`-l `vud`+s); *estos xx mil de
  agora* (`vom`+s `cad pol`-l `xig` a-h-o-r-a); *poderlo hazer, porque* (p-o `xig` r `pac` `suf`-z-e-r `lu`-`ho`); *las
  quales*; *todos los*; *no pude* (`nuf` p-u `xig`); *si no se*; on f. 20v *los suyos … en Francia la parte de*
  (`gim` = *suyos*: Tomokiyo's "suquos?") and *tambien … embaxador que esta en Francia … el rey de Francia … de alli …
  por dos … me*. 
- **Disagreements.** The earlier edition's *darian todos los* (f. 20r l. 22) is *pagarian todos los* here (the first
  sign was taken for `∞` d); `hec` = *Inglaterra* (f. 20r l. 13, "que quien Inglaterra que los") does not stand in the
  decipherment, whose text there is *en poder […] quien quiera q los*: r2i reads `hec` *quiera* (below). *ahora* of the
  cipher (a-h-o-r-a) is *agora* in the decipherment; the reading follows the decipherment's word, the figures give *ahora*.
- **The tear (r2i, 23 Oct 2026).** f. 22r ll. 9-11 lose about ten words. The passage stands in cipher on f. 20r ll.
  11-14 (DECODE image P1, right page), read sign by sign at 2.5x (`R9635_reread_log.md`, r2i): `vog rug vac yig`
  ∂-a#-#-& = *en la dicha capitulacion*; g-4-ao-g-a# `ho` g-`ta` = r-e-p-l-i + *que* + l + *es* = *repliqueles*;
  `ho` g-`ta` `ho rug`+s qa#4fng `vum`+v-a-n `vog` ao-#-`xig`-g = *que les que las tierras estavan en poder*; `xel gub
  rac` g g-`ta` ao-#-& `vu` 4-g-#-& `ho hob hec ho pac`+s = *del señor infante y respondieron que quien quiera que
  los*; ✗ₒ-m-g#-a#-4-Lo-∂ `dom pud` = *tuviese vuestra magestad*. The decipherment's surviving words (*…es que las
  tierras estavan en poder … quien quiera q los … vra. ma. havia prometido*) stand in the same order, so the gap is
  carried by the figures. Two key values follow: `hec` = **quiera** (h-block between `hob` quien and `hed` rey) and
  `vac` = **dicha** (v-block, before `vec` dize, `vic` dicho); and `gta`, the "recurring token" of the edition, is
  the l-sign + `ta` (es) = *les* (also *que les fue[ssen]*, l. 10). The earlier "-ron que quien Inglaterra que los"
  was this passage misread.
- *[?Menorami]* is written *me|norami* across a line break: a correspondent of the Signoria, not identified.
  *[?Lucerna]* reads *delureina* (one stroke short of *Lucerna*); the Swiss levies for Picardy fit it.
Translation

R9635_en.md

# R9635: Alonso Sánchez to Charles V, Venice, 16 September 1522 — English

Translation of `R9635_reading.md` (the contemporary decipherment on f. 22r, checked against the cipher; the torn passage
from the cipher of f. 20r). Same holes.

Seeing that letters from Your Majesty were so long [?already] in coming, and since nothing can be lost by it, I went
to the lords of this Republic and told them that they knew well that by the capitulation of the truce which they have
concluded with Your Majesty they were to pay, on the first of this month, another 20,000 ducats, besides the other
18,000 that they owe from last year; and that I asked them to be pleased to pay them and fulfil what they are bound to.
They answered me what they had answered before: that their lands should be restored to them, as is agreed in the said
capitulation. I replied to them that the lands were in the power of the lord Infante, and they answered that, whoever
might hold them, Your Majesty had promised to restore them. I
told them that it [?should be enough for them] to hold back the first 18,000 ducats until the said lands were restored,
and that they should pay these 20,000 of now. They replied that they could not do it, because they had paid and were
paying every year more than six thousand ducats to their subjects for the said lands, which were worth more than
15,000 ducats a year; that on their being restored they would pay all the 20,000 ducats at once. I could not get them
off this, nor do I believe they will ever pay them unless the said lands are restored to them.

The lords of this Republic have [?had] these days letters from [?Menorami] of the 28th of last month from [?Lucerne],
and he writes that all their people [the Swiss] were holding that for now they would go to serve in France on the side
of Picardy, and that after this whole month no more campaigning could be done in that region, and they will come to
the enterprise of Italy. These lords also have letters from their ambassador in France of the 26th of last month; he
says that the King of France was going from Blois to Paris and from there to Picardy. This has been confirmed to me by
two ways.

R9653, Venice, 20 Nov 1522 (RAH Salazar 9/26 ff. 299-301) decoded, not read

Sánchez to Charles V: the clear middle gives the news of the siege of Rhodes from the Signoria's letters (fifty thousand Turks dead, the defenders hoping for relief); the two cipher lines at the foot are read with the published key; the six-line head paragraph is illegible at DECODE's resolution.

A mechanical decode with holes in most sentences: shown so that the transcription and the key can be checked and improved, not as a reading.

Transcription
